# Seed 55 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - small; the first run checked at 32 bits (Iteration 334)

`seed 55 tag2 6h then experiment compare-fronts.py --rounds 6 --frontier --cpus 2,8`, commit
a29d2fdb7 (Iteration 333) - the after-seed-52 genes a third time, run for the checks: the first with
dream.lan's 32-bit x86 check. The chain 13:26-19:10 UTC Oct 16 (5 h 44 min): the evolution 3 h 42
min, the re-measure 17 min, the comparison at 6 rounds 1 h 28 min. 22,785 designs.

The verdict: fastest +3.1%, smallest +0.00%, memory +0 KB, hypervolume +0.4%, best Q -2.5% -
**small**, the third in a row of these genes. bfind on 80 of the 168 of the front (seed 54: 67 of
195); no corpus deaths; children: mutation 55.3%, crossover 33.1%, borrowed 11.6%.

**The checks** (results/xcheck-dream-seed55.md):
- dream.lan, 64-bit, the laptop's binaries: Spearman **0.940**; the laptop's six fastest 2 5 6 7 1 10.
- dream.lan, **32-bit x86 at 4-byte cells**, the laptop's static builds (interpreted designs only -
  the JIT is x86-64's): Spearman **0.694**; the laptop's six fastest **8 10 9 11 13 12** there.
  The slow designs keep their place (here/laptop 0.94-1.04); the fast ones lose about half their
  lead over s6 (de90232b56: 0.368 on the laptop, 0.700 there) - the skew the owner feared, measured.
  It mixes the machine with the width; but dream agrees with the laptop at 64 bits (0.940), and the
  only fast design in both samples, c0e1076353, is 8% slower on dream at 64 bits and 15% more at 32.
  The two samples share 8 designs, nearly all slow and within 5% of each other - their ranks noise.
  Next: a 64-bit check on dream from the same pool as the 32-bit one, the width alone.
- rage.lan: failed - build-stages.sh stopped on engine/pack4.c's sysc guard: the 32-bit work ported
  vm-lab.c and relf.c, and four hand-made engines kept the x86-only guard (fixed: the pending batch).

Held out (heldout.py):
  sieve (held out): 168 designs; Spearman with the selection speed 0.959
    the 10 fastest by selection, their rank on it: 1 8 5 2 14 6 9 15 10 11
    held-out / selection speed: min 0.39, median 0.58, max 2.73
    flattered most by the selection: af27644082 2.73, c85e419ec0 2.62, cf07ae9ce3 2.60, 595dd8ef00 2.05, 875c03c677 2.01

---

# Evolved VM designs

17324 designs evaluated, 17225 alive, 5461 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): 0212434545, speed 0.147, binary + image 27,921, memory 160 KB - Q 0.200 of s6-cv8b's.

## The front's genomes

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.

```jsonl
{"bin": 23252, "etext": null, "gen": 49, "genome": {"abortif": 0, "align1": 1, "alignfn": 1, "alignhot": 0, "bfind": 1, "bss": 1, "bytehdr": 1, "compiler": 1, "d256": 0, "doesfar": 1, "dskew": 1, "enc": "cv8", "escape": 2, "factor": 2, "fbuf": 0, "fold": 1, "folds": ["AND", "!", "C@", "@", "SWAP", "LIT", "XOR", "OR", "RSHIFT"], "fsys": 2, "gotodup": 0, "guard": 0, "hotcalls": 16, "hotinl": 10, "ipaclone": 0, "jit": 1, "jitinl": 1, "jitk": 3, "jitloop": 1, "jitos": 0, "jittail": 1, "jitx": 1, "kfast": 1, "kinput": 1, "klookup": 1, "knumber": 1, "kstack": 0, "lean": 1, "lto": 1, "msc": 0,
```
